      "quote": "Es heißt, dass er ein aus dem Himmel verstoßener Engel sei und eine Vogelstimme habe. Er hatte sich aber nicht versündigt wie die anderen Gefallenen und hoffte so, dass ihm Gnade gewährt würde. So versteckte er sich mehr denn tausend Jahre in einem Grashalm im Moor und wartete auf eine sich bietende Gelegenheit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikatakan malaikat yang terusir dari surga dan bersuara burung; ia tidak berdosa seperti malaikat jatuh lainnya dan berharap diampuni, sehingga lebih dari seribu tahun bersembunyi di sehelai rumput di rawa menunggu kesempatan, lalu mengikuti seorang pelayan ke Sachsenheim.",
        "en": "He is said to be an angel cast out of heaven with a bird's voice; not having sinned like the other fallen, he hoped for mercy, so he hid over a thousand years in a blade of grass in the moor awaiting his chance, then followed a servant to Sachsenheim."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c03",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "Alle dienstbarkaiten und was im schloß zu thuon gewest, das hat er ganz williglichen, so im ds befolchen worden, verricht. Er hat den Leuten vorgezündt mit liechter, kartenspill und anders, was er gehaißen worden, gebracht. Solchs hat man im Luft sehen daher geen und niemands, der das getragen, sehen künden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zimmerische Chronik: ia mengerjakan dengan sukarela semua tugas di kastel (schloß) yang diperintahkan kepadanya, menerangi jalan orang dengan lilin, membawa kartu dan barang lain; benda-benda itu terlihat melayang di udara tanpa ada yang tampak membawanya.",
        "en": "Zimmern Chronicle: he willingly did every task in the castle (schloß) he was bidden, lighting people's way with candles, fetching cards and other things; these were seen moving through the air with no visible bearer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c04",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "Er wird als fürchterlich anzusehen beschrieben, so wollte einst der Herr Reinhard von Sachsenheim den Kobold einmal sehen und obwohl der Geist ablehnte, drängte der Herr ihn, bis er ihm seinen Arm zeigte",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan mengerikan; Reinhard von Sachsenheim ingin melihat kobold itu, dan meski roh itu menolak, ia terus mendesak sampai roh itu memperlihatkan lengannya.",
        "en": "He was described as dreadful to see; Reinhard von Sachsenheim wanted to see the kobold, and though the spirit refused, he pressed until it showed him its arm."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c05",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "Als der Herr Reinhard auf Drängen seiner Freunde alsbald den gräßlichen, aber dennoch hilfsbereiten, Geist beschwören und bannen ließ, verhängte dieser einen Fluch über das Geschlecht der Sachsenheim, der das Ende der Familie bedeuten sollte. Bereits kurz darauf brannte das gesamte Anwesen vollständig nieder",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika Reinhard, atas desakan teman-temannya, menyuruh roh yang mengerikan namun suka menolong itu diusir dengan mantra, roh itu mengutuk keluarga Sachsenheim, kutukan yang berarti akhir keluarga itu; tak lama kemudian seluruh kastel terbakar habis.",
        "en": "When Reinhard, urged by friends, had the ghastly yet helpful spirit exorcised and banned, it cursed the Sachsenheim line, a curse meant to end the family; shortly after, the whole estate burned down completely."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c06",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "sonder auch das uralt geschlecht ist in wenig jaren abgestorben, welches doch bei unsern vorfarn in hochem ansehen gewesen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Zimmerische Chronik: bukan hanya kastel itu yang terbakar, tetapi keluarga yang sangat tua itu juga punah dalam beberapa tahun.",
        "en": "Zimmern Chronicle: not only did the castle burn, but the ancient family died out within a few years."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c07",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "Alle gebett, was man ime vorgesprochen, das hat er nachgesagt, auch den glauben und das vatterunser, ohne die wort, und füer uns nit in böse versuchung, sondern erlös uns von allem übel!' die hat er geschwigen und nit sagen wellen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengulang semua doa yang diucapkan kepadanya, juga Pengakuan Iman dan Doa Bapa Kami, kecuali kata-kata \"janganlah membawa kami ke dalam pencobaan, tetapi lepaskanlah kami dari yang jahat\", yang tak mau ia ucapkan.",
        "en": "He repeated every prayer said before him, the Creed and the Lord's Prayer too, except the words 'lead us not into temptation, but deliver us from evil', which he would not say."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "entenwick-c08",
      "source_id": "entenwick-s1",
      "quote": "Der war mit langen spitzen am ellenbogen und duch auß, die finger aber an der handt auch spitzig und greulich und die farb so erschrockenlich, mit ploe und blutfarb und gelen spreckln also vermischt",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lengan itu berduri panjang di siku dan di sepanjangnya, jari-jarinya runcing dan mengerikan, warnanya menakutkan, bercampur bintik biru (ploe), merah darah, dan kuning.",
        "en": "The arm had long spikes at the elbow and all along, the fingers pointed and ghastly, the colour frightful, mixed with blue (ploe), blood-red and yellow specks."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Freki adalah salah satu dari dua serigala dewa Odin dalam mitologi Nordik, bersama Geri, yang diberi semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla.",
    "en": "Freki is one of the god Odin's two wolves in Norse mythology, together with Geri, who are given all the food served to Odin in Valhalla.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Freki dan Geri adalah dua serigala milik Odin; nama mereka berarti \"yang rakus\" dan \"yang lahap\". Keduanya disebut dalam Grímnismál. Odin duduk di singgasana Hliðskjálf bersama kedua serigala ini dan kedua burung gagak Huginn dan Muninn.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Freki and Geri are Odin's two wolves; their names mean 'the greedy one' and 'the ravenous one'. Both are named in Grímnismál. Odin sits on the throne Hliðskjálf with these two wolves and the two ravens Huginn and Muninn.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "freki-c04",
        "freki-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Serigala-serigala itu menerima semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla, karena Odin hidup dari anggur saja; Odin juga mengirim mereka ke dunia untuk membawakan kabar baginya. Dalam Younger Edda terjemahan Anderson, Har menjawab Ganglere bahwa makanan di meja Odin ia berikan kepada kedua serigalanya, sebab ia sendiri tak memerlukan makanan; anggur adalah makanan sekaligus minumannya.",
      "en": "The wolves receive all the food served to Odin in Valhalla, since Odin lives on wine alone; Odin also sends them out into the world to bring him news. In Anderson's translation of the Younger Edda, Har tells Ganglere that the food placed on Odin's table he gives to his two wolves, needing no food himself; wine is both his food and his drink.",

      "id": "freki-c01",
      "source_id": "freki-s1",
      "quote": "Gere og Freke er i den norrøne mytologien Odins to ulver. Navnene betyr \"den grådige\" og \"den glupske\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (norrøne), Gere (Geri) dan Freke (Freki) adalah dua serigala Odin; nama mereka berarti \"yang rakus\" dan \"yang lahap\".",
        "en": "In Norse (norrøne) mythology Gere (Geri) and Freke (Freki) are Odin's two wolves; their names mean 'the greedy one' and 'the ravenous one'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "freki-c02",
      "source_id": "freki-s1",
      "quote": "Ulvene får all mat som Odin serveres i Valhall, siden han lever av vin alene. Odin sender dem ut i verden for å hente nytt til ham.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serigala-serigala itu mendapat semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla (Valhall), karena ia hidup dari anggur saja; Odin mengutus mereka ke dunia untuk membawakan kabar.",
        "en": "The wolves get all the food served to Odin in Valhalla (Valhall), since he lives on wine alone; Odin sends them out into the world to fetch news for him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "freki-c03",
      "source_id": "freki-s2",
      "quote": "Ganglere asked: Does Odin have the same kind of food as the einherjes? Har answered: The food that is placed on his table he gives to his two wolves, which hight Gere and Freke. He needs no food himself. Wine is to him both food and drink",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Younger Edda (terjemahan Anderson), Ganglere bertanya apakah Odin makan makanan yang sama dengan para einherjes; Har menjawab bahwa makanan di meja Odin ia berikan kepada kedua serigalanya, Gere dan Freke; ia sendiri tak butuh makanan, anggur adalah makanan sekaligus minumannya.",
        "en": "In the Younger Edda (Anderson's translation), Ganglere asks whether Odin eats the same food as the einherjes; Har answers that the food placed on his table he gives to his two wolves, Gere and Freke; he needs no food himself, wine being both food and drink to him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "freki-c04",
      "source_id": "freki-s1",
      "quote": "Den norrøne guden Odin sitter på trona Lidskjalv med ulvene Gere og Freke og ravnene Hugin og Munin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa Nordik Odin duduk di singgasana Hliðskjálf (Lidskjalv) bersama serigala Gere dan Freke serta gagak Huginn (Hugin) dan Muninn (Munin).",
        "en": "The Norse god Odin sits on the throne Hliðskjálf (Lidskjalv) with the wolves Gere and Freke and the ravens Huginn (Hugin) and Muninn (Munin)."
      }
    },

        "en": "The wolves are named in Grímnismál (Grimnismål)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gajendra adalah gajah penting dalam mitologi Hindu, tokoh utama kisah Gajendra Moksha dalam Bhagavata Purana, yang diselamatkan Wisnu dari cengkeraman buaya berkat doanya.",
    "en": "Gajendra is an important elephant of Hindu mythology, hero of the Gajendra Moksha story in the Bhagavata Purana, saved by Vishnu from a crocodile's grip thanks to his prayer.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gajendra adalah gajah penting dalam mitologi Hindu yang kisahnya diwariskan dalam kitab kedelapan Bhagavata Purana. Ia tokoh utama kisah Gajendra Moksha (\"pembebasan raja gajah\"), yang dalam agama Hindu dianggap teladan penting bhakti, yaitu pengabdian tanpa syarat, dan rahmat ilahi. Menurut tradisi, dalam kehidupan sebelumnya ia raja bernama Indradyumna, pemuja Wisnu, yang karena kutukan resi Agastya terlahir kembali sebagai gajah tetapi tetap mempertahankan kecenderungan spiritualnya.",
      "en": "Gajendra is an important elephant of Hindu mythology whose story is handed down in the eighth book of the Bhagavata Purana. He is the hero of the tale known as Gajendra Moksha ('the liberation of the elephant king'), regarded in Hinduism as a key example of bhakti, unconditional devotion, and divine grace. According to tradition, in an earlier life he was a king named Indradyumna, a devotee of Vishnu, who through a curse of the sage Agastya was reborn as an elephant but kept his spiritual inclination.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gajendra-c01",
        "gajendra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagai pemimpin kawanannya, Gajendra hidup di taman surgawi Rtumat di Gunung Trikuta. Saat mengunjungi sebuah danau, ia diserang buaya raksasa (makara) yang mencengkeram kakinya, dan menurut legenda pergulatan itu berlangsung lebih dari seribu tahun. Ketika menyadari kekuatannya sendiri tak cukup, ia berdoa kepada Wisnu sambil mengangkat belalai berisi bunga teratai sebagai persembahan; Wisnu datang di atas Garuda dan membunuh buaya itu dengan cakra Sudarshana.",
      "en": "As leader of his herd, Gajendra lived in the heavenly garden Rtumat on Mount Trikuta. While visiting a lake he was seized by the leg by a huge crocodile (makara), and according to legend the struggle lasted more than a thousand years. Realising his own strength was not enough, he prayed to Vishnu, raising his trunk with a lotus flower as an offering; Vishnu appeared on Garuda and killed the crocodile with the Sudarshana Chakra.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gajendra-c03",
        "gajendra-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Berkat pengabdian penuhnya kepada Wisnu, Gajendra mencapai moksha, pembebasan dari lingkaran kelahiran kembali, memperoleh wujud ilahi, dan memasuki Vaikuntha. Ia menjadi motif yang sering dijumpai dalam seni Hindu, terutama pahatan dan lukisan di kuil-kuil Wisnu.",
      "en": "Through his complete devotion to Vishnu, Gajendra attained moksha, liberation from the cycle of rebirth, gained a divine form and entered Vaikuntha. He is a frequent motif in Hindu art, especially in sculptures and paintings in Vishnu temples.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gajendra-c05",
        "gajendra-c06"
      ]
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga Wasmes adalah naga dalam legenda Borinage, Belgia, yang meneror penduduk Wasmes sekitar tahun 1130 sampai dikalahkan Gilles de Chin, yang membebaskan gadis kecil Pucelette.",
    "en": "The dragon of Wasmes is a dragon of a legend of the Borinage, Belgium, that terrorised the people of Wasmes around 1130 until it was defeated by Gilles de Chin, who freed the little girl Pucelette.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gayant-c01",
      "gayant-c02",
      "gayant-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga Wasmes adalah tokoh legenda dari Borinage, Belgia, tentang pertarungan Gilles de Chin melawan naga yang meneror penduduk Wasmes. Menurut legenda, sekitar tahun 1130 seekor binatang raksasa menghantui rawa-rawa Wasmes; ia menebar ketakutan di seluruh Borinage meski tak ada yang benar-benar melihatnya, menyerang ternak, dan melahap korbannya. Suatu hari ia menyerang gadis kecil berusia empat atau lima tahun, Pucelette, dan menawannya di sarangnya.",
      "en": "The dragon of Wasmes is the subject of a legend from the Borinage, Belgium, about Gilles de Chin's fight with a dragon that terrorised the people of Wasmes. The legend says that around 1130 a colossal beast haunted the marshes of Wasmes; it spread fear throughout the Borinage though no one had truly seen it, attacked livestock and devoured its victims. One day it seized a little girl of four or five, the Pucelette, and held her captive in its lair.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gayant-c01",
        "gayant-c02",
        "gayant-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gilles de Chin, chambellan turun-temurun Hainaut, berdoa memohon bimbingan Bunda Maria lalu berangkat berkuda dengan tombak. Ketika ia tiba di rawa, binatang itu keluar dari guanya sambil menyemburkan api; setelah beberapa tusukan tombak, Gilles menang, masuk ke sarang naga, dan membawa Pucelette kembali ke Wasmes, sedangkan kepala naga dibawa ke Mons. Legenda ini dirayakan setiap Senin Pentakosta dalam arak-arakan Tour de Wasmes, dan mirip dengan legenda Santo Georgius.",
      "en": "Gilles de Chin, hereditary chamberlain of Hainaut, prayed to Our Lady for guidance and set off on horseback with a lance. When he reached the marsh the beast came out of its cave spitting fire; after several lance thrusts Gilles won, entered the dragon's den and brought the Pucelette back to Wasmes, while the dragon's head was taken to Mons. The legend is celebrated each Whit Monday in the Tour de Wasmes procession, and resembles the legend of Saint George.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gayant-c04",
        "gayant-c05",
        "gayant-c06",
        "gayant-c07",
        "gayant-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "gayant-c02",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "La légende raconte qu'aux alentours de l'an 1130, une colossale bête hantait les marais de Wasmes. Elle semait la crainte dans tout le Borinage alors que personne ne l'avait réellement vue. Elle s'attaquait au bétail et dévorait ses victimes.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",

        "id": "Menurut legenda, sekitar tahun 1130 seekor binatang raksasa menghantui rawa-rawa Wasmes, menebar ketakutan di seluruh Borinage meski tak ada yang benar-benar melihatnya, menyerang ternak, dan melahap korbannya.",

    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c03",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "Un jour, le dragon s'attaqua à une petite fille âgée de 4 ou 5 ans, la Pucelette. Il la retenait prisonnière dans sa tanière.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "One day the dragon attacked a little girl of four or five, the Pucelette, and held her prisoner in its lair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c04",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "Quand Gilles arriva dans les marais où se terrait la bête, celle-ci sentit sa présence et sortit de sa grotte en crachant du feu. Nul doute, c'était un dragon. Il s'ensuit un combat dont Gilles sortit vainqueur après plusieurs coups de lance. Il entra dans l'antre du Dragon et ramena la Pucelette à Wasmes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika Gilles (Gilles de Chin) tiba di rawa, binatang itu merasakan kehadirannya dan keluar dari guanya sambil menyemburkan api, jelas seekor naga; setelah beberapa tusukan tombak Gilles menang, masuk ke sarang naga, dan membawa Pucelette kembali ke Wasmes.",
        "en": "When Gilles (Gilles de Chin) reached the marsh, the beast sensed him and came out of its cave spitting fire, clearly a dragon; after several lance blows Gilles won, entered the dragon's den and brought the Pucelette back to Wasmes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c05",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "Dans le folklore wasmois, cette légende est mise à l'honneur lors du lundi de Pentecôte. Ce jour-là, la Pucelette est symbolisée par une fillette choisie dans la région et est présentée aux habitants de Wasmes. Il s'y déroule une procession, le Tour de Wasmes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam folklor Wasmes, legenda ini dirayakan pada Senin Pentakosta (lundi de Pentecôte): Pucelette diperankan gadis kecil setempat dan diperkenalkan kepada warga, dan diadakan arak-arakan Tour de Wasmes.",
        "en": "In Wasmes folklore the legend is honoured on Whit Monday (lundi de Pentecôte): the Pucelette is played by a local little girl presented to the townsfolk, and the Tour de Wasmes procession is held."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c06",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "La légende du dragon de Wasmes comporte certaines similitudes avec d'autres légendes, notamment celle de saint Georges qui terrasse un dragon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Naga Wasmes memiliki kemiripan dengan legenda lain, terutama legenda Santo Georgius (saint Georges) yang menaklukkan naga.",
        "en": "The dragon of Wasmes legend has similarities with others, notably that of Saint George (saint Georges) slaying a dragon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c07",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "Avant de partir en expédition, il invoque Notre-Dame et lui demande de le guider. Il s'en va chevauchant un cheval et armé d'une lance.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebelum berangkat, Gilles de Chin, chambellan Hainaut, berdoa kepada Bunda Maria (Notre-Dame) memohon bimbingan, lalu pergi berkuda dengan tombak.",
        "en": "Before setting out, Gilles de Chin, chamberlain of Hainaut, invoked Our Lady (Notre-Dame) for guidance, then rode off armed with a lance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gayant-c08",
      "source_id": "gayant-s1",
      "quote": "La tête du dragon fut amenée à Mons.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepala naga itu dibawa ke Mons.",
        "en": "The dragon's head was taken to Mons."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

Artikel fr bertanda kurang sumber sekunder; tidak ada sumber kedua."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Geri adalah salah satu dari dua serigala dewa Odin dalam mitologi Nordik, bersama Freki, yang diberi semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla.",
    "en": "Geri is one of the god Odin's two wolves in Norse mythology, together with Freki, who are given all the food served to Odin in Valhalla.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "geri-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Geri dan Freki adalah dua serigala milik Odin; nama mereka berarti \"yang rakus\" dan \"yang lahap\". Keduanya disebut dalam Grímnismál. Odin duduk di singgasana Hliðskjálf bersama kedua serigala ini dan kedua burung gagak Huginn dan Muninn.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Geri and Freki are Odin's two wolves; their names mean 'the greedy one' and 'the ravenous one'. Both are named in Grímnismál. Odin sits on the throne Hliðskjálf with these two wolves and the two ravens Huginn and Muninn.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "geri-c01",
        "geri-c04",
        "geri-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Serigala-serigala itu menerima semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla, karena Odin hidup dari anggur saja; Odin juga mengirim mereka ke dunia untuk membawakan kabar baginya. Dalam Younger Edda terjemahan Anderson, Har menjawab Ganglere bahwa makanan di meja Odin ia berikan kepada kedua serigalanya, sebab ia sendiri tak memerlukan makanan; anggur adalah makanan sekaligus minumannya.",
      "en": "The wolves receive all the food served to Odin in Valhalla, since Odin lives on wine alone; Odin also sends them out into the world to bring him news. In Anderson's translation of the Younger Edda, Har tells Ganglere that the food placed on Odin's table he gives to his two wolves, needing no food himself; wine is both his food and his drink.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "geri-c01",
      "source_id": "geri-s1",
      "quote": "Gere og Freke er i den norrøne mytologien Odins to ulver. Navnene betyr \"den grådige\" og \"den glupske\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik (norrøne), Gere (Geri) dan Freke (Freki) adalah dua serigala Odin; nama mereka berarti \"yang rakus\" dan \"yang lahap\".",
        "en": "In Norse (norrøne) mythology Gere (Geri) and Freke (Freki) are Odin's two wolves; their names mean 'the greedy one' and 'the ravenous one'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geri-c02",
      "source_id": "geri-s1",
      "quote": "Ulvene får all mat som Odin serveres i Valhall, siden han lever av vin alene. Odin sender dem ut i verden for å hente nytt til ham.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Serigala-serigala itu mendapat semua makanan yang disajikan kepada Odin di Valhalla (Valhall), karena ia hidup dari anggur saja; Odin mengutus mereka ke dunia untuk membawakan kabar.",
        "en": "The wolves get all the food served to Odin in Valhalla (Valhall), since he lives on wine alone; Odin sends them out into the world to fetch news for him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geri-c03",
      "source_id": "geri-s2",
      "quote": "Ganglere asked: Does Odin have the same kind of food as the einherjes? Har answered: The food that is placed on his table he gives to his two wolves, which hight Gere and Freke. He needs no food himself. Wine is to him both food and drink",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Younger Edda (terjemahan Anderson), Ganglere bertanya apakah Odin makan makanan yang sama dengan para einherjes; Har menjawab bahwa makanan di meja Odin ia berikan kepada kedua serigalanya, Gere dan Freke; ia sendiri tak butuh makanan, anggur adalah makanan sekaligus minumannya.",
        "en": "In the Younger Edda (Anderson's translation), Ganglere asks whether Odin eats the same food as the einherjes; Har answers that the food placed on his table he gives to his two wolves, Gere and Freke; he needs no food himself, wine being both food and drink to him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geri-c04",
      "source_id": "geri-s1",
      "quote": "Den norrøne guden Odin sitter på trona Lidskjalv med ulvene Gere og Freke og ravnene Hugin og Munin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewa Nordik Odin duduk di singgasana Hliðskjálf (Lidskjalv) bersama serigala Gere dan Freke serta gagak Huginn (Hugin) dan Muninn (Munin).",
        "en": "The Norse god Odin sits on the throne Hliðskjálf (Lidskjalv) with the wolves Gere and Freke and the ravens Huginn (Hugin) and Muninn (Munin)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "geri-c05",
      "source_id": "geri-s1",
      "quote": "De nevnes i Grimnismål («Grimnnes tale»):",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua serigala disebut dalam Grímnismál (Grimnismål).",
        "en": "The wolves are named in Grímnismál (Grimnismål)."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gloeiige adalah hantu atau penampakan bercahaya dalam kepercayaan rakyat yang terlihat pada malam hari di rawa dan padang lembap, arwah pendosa yang bergentayangan untuk menebus kesalahannya.",
    "en": "The gloeiige is a ghost or glowing apparition of folk belief seen at night in marshes and wet heaths, the soul of a sinner who haunts the land to atone for a wrong.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gloeiige-c01",
      "gloeiige-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gloeiige adalah hantu atau penampakan dalam kepercayaan rakyat yang muncul dalam banyak legenda, sosok yang terlihat pada malam hari di rawa dan padang yang becek. Ia juga disebut vuurman (manusia api), dan ada pula kisah tentang bola-bola api. Banyak legendanya berasal dari Maasland dan Kempen.",
      "en": "The gloeiige is a ghost or apparition of folk belief found in many legends, a figure seen at night in marshes and soggy heaths. It is also called vuurman (fire man), and there are also tales of balls of fire. Many of its legends come from the Maasland and the Kempen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gloeiige-c01",
        "gloeiige-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita, gloeiige kadang pencuri atau prajurit (Inggris) terkutuk yang pernah membunuh seseorang, atau petani yang curang dengan patok batas dan kadang bersumpah palsu tentangnya. Setelah mati mereka harus bergentayangan dan berusaha memperbaiki kesalahan agar mendapat ketenangan. Di Boxtel, misalnya, seorang lelaki bercahaya memanggul patok batas sambil berseru \"di mana harus kuletakkan?\", dan lenyap selamanya setelah seorang pemabuk menyuruhnya menaruh patok itu di tempat asalnya.",
      "en": "According to the tales, a gloeiige is sometimes a cursed thief or (English) soldier who had killed someone, or a farmer who cheated with boundary posts and sometimes swore falsely about it. After death they must haunt and try to set their wrong right in order to find rest. In Boxtel, for instance, a glowing man carried a boundary post on his back calling 'where shall I put it?', and vanished forever after a drunkard told him to put it back where he had taken it from.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gloeiige-c02",
        "gloeiige-c03",
        "gloeiige-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penjelasan penampakan ini biasanya dicari pada gas rawa yang memancarkan cahaya atau kawanan kunang-kunang.",
      "en": "These sightings are usually explained as light-emitting marsh gas or swarms of glow-worms.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gloeiige-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [

      "id": "gloeiige-c01",
      "source_id": "gloeiige-s1",
      "quote": "De gloeiige is een geest of spookverschijning uit het volksgeloof die in diverse sagen voorkomt. Het was volgens de volksverhalen een gedaante die 's nachts werd waargenomen in moerassen en drassige heidevelden. Een gloeiige wordt ook wel vuurman genoemd, ook is er sprake van vuurballen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gloeiige adalah hantu atau penampakan dalam kepercayaan rakyat (volksgeloof) yang muncul dalam banyak legenda, sosok yang terlihat malam hari di rawa dan padang becek; ia juga disebut vuurman, dan ada pula bola-bola api (vuurballen).",
        "en": "The gloeiige is a ghost or apparition of folk belief (volksgeloof) in many legends, a figure seen at night in marshes and wet heaths; it is also called vuurman, and there are fireballs (vuurballen) too."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gloeiige-c02",
      "source_id": "gloeiige-s1",
      "quote": "Er bestaan vele sagen hieromtrent, waarvan veel uit het Maasland en de Kempen. Volgens de verhalen is de gloeiige soms een vervloekte dief of (Engelse) soldaat die iemand vermoord heeft. Ook andere personen die een moord begingen, worden genoemd. Ook een boer die gefraudeerd had met grenspalen (en soms bij het getuigen daarover meineed had begaan), wordt soms ten tonele gevoerd.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak legenda tentangnya berasal dari Maasland dan Kempen; gloeiige kadang pencuri atau prajurit (Inggris; Engelse) terkutuk yang membunuh seseorang, pembunuh lain, atau petani yang curang dengan patok batas (grenspalen) dan kadang bersumpah palsu tentangnya.",
        "en": "Many legends come from the Maasland and the Kempen; the gloeiige is sometimes a cursed thief or (English; Engelse) soldier who killed someone, another murderer, or a farmer who cheated with boundary posts (grenspalen) and sometimes perjured himself about it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gloeiige-c03",
      "source_id": "gloeiige-s1",
      "quote": "Na de dood moeten de gloeiigen rondspoken en doen ze pogingen om hun fout te herstellen om rust te krijgen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah mati, para gloeiige harus bergentayangan dan berusaha memperbaiki kesalahan mereka agar mendapat ketenangan.",
        "en": "After death the gloeiigen must haunt and try to set their wrong right to find rest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gloeiige-c04",
      "source_id": "gloeiige-s1",
      "quote": "In Boxtel liep tussen twaalf en een (het spookuur) een man in een hel licht in het Meulenbroek. Hij draagt een grenspaal op zijn rug en roept altijd: waar zal ik hem laten. Een dronkaard zegt op een dag dat hij hem moet plaatsen, waar hij hem vandaan heeft gehaald. De geest is nooit meer gezien.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Boxtel, antara pukul dua belas dan satu, seorang lelaki bercahaya terang berjalan di Meulenbroek memanggul patok batas sambil berseru \"di mana harus kuletakkan\"; suatu hari seorang pemabuk menyuruhnya menaruhnya di tempat asal, dan hantu itu tak pernah terlihat lagi.",
        "en": "In Boxtel, between twelve and one, a man in bright light walked the Meulenbroek carrying a boundary post on his back, always calling 'where shall I put it'; one day a drunkard told him to put it where he had taken it, and the ghost was never seen again."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gloeiige-c05",
      "source_id": "gloeiige-s1",
      "quote": "De verklaring van deze waarnemingen wordt doorgaans gezocht in moerasgassen die licht uitstralen, dan wel in wolken glimwormpjes.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjelasan penampakan ini biasanya dicari pada gas rawa (moerasgassen) yang memancarkan cahaya, atau kawanan kunang-kunang (glimwormpjes).",
        "en": "These sightings are usually explained as light-emitting marsh gases (moerasgassen) or clouds of glow-worms (glimwormpjes)."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Harpyia adalah anjing betina pemburu Aktaion dalam mitologi Yunani, yang ikut mencabik tuannya setelah Artemis mengubahnya menjadi rusa.",
    "en": "Harpyia is one of Actaeon's hunting bitches in Greek mythology, who helped tear her master apart after Artemis turned him into a stag.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "harpyia-c01",
      "harpyia-c02"
    ]

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani, Harpyia (Ἃρπυια) adalah anjing betina milik Aktaion. Aktaion adalah pemburu yang suatu hari tanpa sengaja memergoki dewi Artemis (Diana) sedang mandi; sang dewi mengubahnya menjadi rusa, yang kemudian dicabik anjing-anjing pemburunya sendiri, termasuk Harpyia.",
      "en": "In Greek mythology Harpyia (Ἃρπυια) is one of Actaeon's hounds. Actaeon was a hunter who one day accidentally came upon the goddess Artemis (Diana) bathing; she changed him into a stag, which was then torn apart by his own hunting dogs, Harpyia among them.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "harpyia-c01",
        "harpyia-c02"

      "id": "Harpyia disebut dalam tiga versi legenda Aktaion, yaitu oleh Aiskhylos, Ovidius, dan Hyginus; menurut Metamorphoses karya Ovidius, ia ditemani dua anaknya. Namanya mungkin terilhami harpy, makhluk campuran burung yang dalam mitologi Yunani sering membunuh manusia, dan catatan terjemahan Riley mengartikannya \"si pemangsa\".",
      "en": "Harpyia is mentioned in three versions of the Actaeon legend, by Aeschylus, Ovid and Hyginus; according to Ovid's Metamorphoses two of her whelps accompanied her. Her name may be inspired by the harpies, bird-hybrid creatures that often killed people in Greek mythology, and the notes to Riley's translation gloss it as 'Ravener'.",

      "locator": null,

      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Yunani (griechischen), Harpyia (Ἃρπυια) adalah anjing betina Aktaion.",

    },
    {
      "id": "harpyia-c02",
      "source_id": "harpyia-s1",
      "quote": "Aktaion war ein mythologischer Jäger, der eines Tages unabsichtlich die Göttin Artemis (bzw. deren römische Entsprechung, Diana) beim Bade überraschte. Diese verwandelte ihn daraufhin in einen Hirsch, der dann von seinen eigenen Jagdhunden, darunter Harpyia, zerfleischt wurde.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aktaion adalah pemburu mitologis yang tanpa sengaja memergoki dewi Artemis (Diana) sedang mandi; sang dewi mengubahnya menjadi rusa, yang lalu dicabik anjing-anjing pemburunya sendiri, termasuk Harpyia.",
        "en": "Actaeon (Aktaion) was a mythical hunter who accidentally surprised the goddess Artemis (Diana) bathing; she turned him into a stag, which was then torn apart by his own hounds, Harpyia among them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "harpyia-c03",
      "source_id": "harpyia-s1",
      "quote": "Harpyia wird in drei Versionen der Sage des Aktaion erwähnt: bei Aischylos, Ovid und Hyginus. Laut Ovids Metamorphosen begleiteten sie zwei ihrer Söhne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Harpyia disebut dalam tiga versi legenda Aktaion: oleh Aiskhylos (Aischylos), Ovidius (Ovid), dan Hyginus; menurut Metamorphoses karya Ovidius ia ditemani dua anaknya.",
        "en": "Harpyia is named in three versions of the Actaeon legend: by Aeschylus (Aischylos), Ovid and Hyginus; per Ovid's Metamorphoses two of her sons accompanied her."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "harpyia-c04",
      "source_id": "harpyia-s1",
      "quote": "Der Name der Harpyia mag von den Harpyien inspiriert sein, Vogelmischwesen, die in der griechischen Mythologie häufig Menschen töteten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Harpyia mungkin terilhami harpy (Harpyien), makhluk campuran burung yang dalam mitologi Yunani sering membunuh manusia.",
        "en": "Harpyia's name may be inspired by the harpies (Harpyien), bird-hybrid beings that often killed people in Greek mythology."
      }
    },

      "source_id": "harpyia-s2",
      "quote": "Ver. 215. ‘Ravener.’ From the Greek word",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Heimchen adalah makhluk dari cerita rakyat Jerman (German folklore) dengan beberapa makna yang saling berkaitan: jangkrik rumah, kurcaci kecil, dan arwah anak-anak dalam rombongan Perchta.",
    "en": "The Heimchen is a being from German folklore with several related meanings: the house cricket, tiny dwarf-like folk, and child souls in the train of Perchta.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "heimchen-c01",
      "heimchen-c02",
      "heimchen-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Heimchen (bentuk kecil dari Heim, rumah) pada dasarnya adalah sebutan Jerman untuk jangkrik rumah. Jangkrik rumah merupakan salah satu wujud hewan yang diambil para kurcaci, sebagaimana tampak dari nama-nama dialek seperti Herdschmiedl (pandai besi perapian) dan Heunemänken. Di North Palatinate dan Western Palatinate jangkrik itu, yang di sana disebut Krikelmaus, dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak; di Pomerania, Heimchen justru memberi makan anak yang tersesat dengan roti dan susu; di Silesia, derik jangkrik rumah menandakan hadirnya arwah orang mati. Dalam kepercayaan rakyat, jangkrik di dalam rumah juga dianggap pembawa keberuntungan.",
      "en": "Heimchen (a diminutive of Heim, home) is first of all the German word for the house cricket. The house cricket is one of the animal forms dwarves take, as dialect names such as Herdschmiedl (hearth smith) and Heunemänken show. In North Palatinate and Western Palatinate the cricket, called Krikelmaus there, is a bogey to frighten children; in Pomerania the Heimchen instead feed lost children with bread rolls and milk; in Silesia the chirping house cricket signals that a dead soul is present. In folk belief a cricket in the house was also held to bring luck.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c02",
        "heimchen-c03",
        "heimchen-c04",
        "heimchen-c05",
        "heimchen-c14",
        "heimchen-c15"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Vogtland, Heimchen adalah makhluk kecil yang tingginya tak sampai dua kaki. Mereka tinggal di padang luas di dalam gua gunung yang terang seperti siang karena sebutir batu karbunkel besar. Pintu masuknya tidak selalu terbuka, dan Heimchen, yang menyebut diri Bergvolk, tidak selalu terlihat oleh mata manusia. Mereka menggembalakan domba emas sebesar anak domba, dijaga Ilsa, gadis berbaju putih yang terkena sihir dan menanti penebusannya.",
      "en": "In the Vogtland the Heimchen are small beings not even two feet tall. They live on a wide meadow inside a mountain cave made bright as day by a large carbuncle stone. The entrance is not always open, and the Heimchen, who call themselves Bergvolk, cannot always be seen by human eyes. They herd golden sheep the size of lambs, tended by Ilsa, a white-clad enchanted girl awaiting her release.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c06",
        "heimchen-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Thuringia, Heimchen adalah arwah anak-anak yang belum dibaptis dan meratap, yang mengikuti Perchta sebagai pemimpin mereka; dalam satu kisah setempat Perchta disebut Heimchenkönigin. Di Orlagau, mereka juga disebut Heimele, Butzelmännchen, dan Erdmännele, roh tanah setinggi satu jari yang tinggal di lubang tikus di rumah. Mereka memberi tanda lebih dahulu apakah penghuni rumah akan beruntung atau sial, kadang meninggalkan hadiah bila dirawat dengan baik, dan diam-diam membantu manusia mengurus ladang, ternak, atau anak yang ditinggal orang tuanya.",
      "en": "In the lore of Thuringia the Heimchen are the wailing souls of unbaptized children, who follow Perchta as their leader; one local tale calls her Heimchenkönigin. In the Orlagau they are also called Heimele, Butzelmännchen and Erdmännele, earth spirits a finger tall living in the mouse holes of houses. They foretell whether the household will have good or bad luck, sometimes leave gifts if treated well, and secretly help people with fields, cattle or children left alone by their parents.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c08",
        "heimchen-c09",
        "heimchen-c10",
        "heimchen-c11",
        "heimchen-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebuah legenda menceritakan bahwa Heimchen dahulu rajin membantu petani di desa-desa Orlagau hingga daerah itu sangat makmur. Lalu datang seorang lelaki dari jauh yang berkata bahwa Perchta tak dapat dipercaya karena Heimchen adalah arwah anak yang belum dibaptis. Ketika orang-orang mulai menjauhi mereka, Perchta memutuskan pergi, dan ia bersama Heimchen menyeberangi sungai dan tak pernah kembali.",
      "en": "One legend tells that the Heimchen once worked hard for the farmers of the Orlagau villages, making the area very rich. Then a man came from far away and said Perchta could not be trusted, because the Heimchen were the souls of unbaptized children. When people began to shun them, Perchta chose to leave, and she and the Heimchen crossed the stream and never came back.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c13"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": {
    "who": {
      "id": "Makhluk cerita rakyat Jerman dengan beberapa makna.",
      "en": "A German folklore being with several meanings.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c01"
      ]
    },
    "origin": {
      "id": "Juga nama Jerman untuk jangkrik rumah.",
      "en": "Also the German name of the house cricket.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c02",
        "heimchen-c15"
      ]
    },
    "role": {
      "id": "Roh tanah sekecil jari di lubang tikus rumah.",
      "en": "Finger-sized earth spirits in house mouse holes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heimchen-c10"
      ]
    },
    "famous_for": {
      "id": "Pengikut Perchta.",

  "did_you_know": {
    "id": "Di Thuringia diceritakan Heimchen pergi selamanya bersama Perchta setelah warga menjauhi mereka karena dianggap arwah anak yang belum dibaptis.",
    "en": "A Thuringian legend says the Heimchen left for good with Perchta after villagers shunned them as souls of unbaptized children.",

  "sources": [
    {

    {
      "id": "heimchen-s2",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heimchen",
      "title": "Heimchen",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "de",

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c01",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "The Heimchen ... is a being from German folklore with several related meanings.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heimchen adalah makhluk dari cerita rakyat Jerman dengan beberapa makna yang saling berkaitan.",
        "en": "The Heimchen is a being from German folklore with several related meanings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c02",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In the first place, Heimchen (diminutive of Heim = home) is the German term for house cricket. The house cricket is one of the animal appearances taken by dwarves, as is also attested by the dialectal names such as Herdschmiedl (hearth smith) and Heunemänken (Mänken = manikin).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heimchen (dari Heim, rumah) adalah sebutan Jerman untuk jangkrik rumah, salah satu wujud hewan kurcaci; nama dialeknya antara lain Herdschmiedl dan Heunemänken.",
        "en": "Heimchen (from Heim, home) is the German term for the house cricket, one of the animal forms of dwarves; dialect names include Herdschmiedl and Heunemänken."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c03",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In North Palatinate and Western Palatinate, the house cricket, there known as Krikelmaus (Maus = mouse), is a nursery bogey used to scare children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di North Palatinate dan Western Palatinate, jangkrik rumah yang disebut Krikelmaus dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak.",
        "en": "In North and Western Palatinate the house cricket, called Krikelmaus, is a nursery bogey to scare children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c04",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In Pomerania, the Heimchen feed lost children with bread rolls and milk instead.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Pomerania, Heimchen memberi makan anak yang tersesat dengan roti dan susu.",
        "en": "In Pomerania the Heimchen feed lost children with bread rolls and milk."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c05",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In Silesia, the stridulating house cricket indicates the presence of a deceased soul.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Silesia, derik jangkrik rumah menandakan hadirnya arwah orang mati.",
        "en": "In Silesia the chirping house cricket indicates a deceased soul is present."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c06",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In the Vogtland, the Heimchen are little beings, nary two feet tall, who dwell on a great meadow inside a mountain cave lit as bright as day by a big carbuncle gem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Vogtland, Heimchen adalah makhluk kecil yang tingginya tak sampai dua kaki, tinggal di padang dalam gua gunung yang diterangi batu karbunkel.",
        "en": "In the Vogtland the Heimchen are little beings under two feet tall living on a meadow inside a mountain cave lit by a carbuncle gem."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c07",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "The entrance is not always open, and the Heimchen or Bergvolk (mountain folk), as they call themselves, are not always visible to human eyes. Those Heimchen keep golden sheep no bigger than lambs whose shepherdess, Ilsa, is an enchanted girl dressed in white with a golden shepherd's staff who is waiting for her redemption.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heimchen atau Bergvolk tidak selalu terlihat oleh mata manusia; mereka memelihara domba emas yang digembalakan Ilsa, gadis tersihir berbaju putih.",
        "en": "The Heimchen or Bergvolk are not always visible to human eyes; they keep golden sheep herded by Ilsa, an enchanted girl in white."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c08",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "The Heimchen are imagined to be the wailing souls of unbaptized children, accompanying Perchta as their leader, according to the lore of Thuringia",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat Thuringia, Heimchen adalah arwah anak-anak belum dibaptis yang mengikuti Perchta sebagai pemimpinnya.",
        "en": "In Thuringian lore the Heimchen are the wailing souls of unbaptized children following Perchta as their leader."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c09",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "She is called Heimchenkönigin, i.e. Heimchen-Queen in one local tale. There is also a belief that a stridulating Heimchen or house cricket sits on the nose of Percht.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah setempat Perchta disebut Heimchenkönigin (ratu Heimchen); ada juga kepercayaan bahwa seekor Heimchen berderik duduk di hidung Percht.",
        "en": "In one local tale she is called Heimchenkönigin (Heimchen-Queen); a belief also holds that a chirping Heimchen sits on the nose of Percht."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c10",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "In the Orlagau in Thuringia the Heimchen, who are also called Heimele, Butzelmännchen (little bogeymen), and Erdmännele (earth manikins), are thought of as tiny earth spirits, as tall as a finger is long, who dwell in the mouse holes of houses.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Orlagau, Thuringia, Heimchen, juga disebut Heimele, Butzelmännchen, dan Erdmännele, dianggap roh tanah setinggi jari yang tinggal di lubang tikus rumah.",
        "en": "In the Orlagau in Thuringia the Heimchen, also called Heimele, Butzelmännchen and Erdmännele, are tiny finger-tall earth spirits living in the mouse holes of houses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c11",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "They indicate beforehand whether the house dwellers will meet good or bad luck and, if well taken care of, at times might also place gifts in front of their mouse hole in the morning.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka memberi tanda lebih dahulu apakah penghuni rumah akan beruntung atau sial, dan bila dirawat baik kadang meninggalkan hadiah.",
        "en": "They indicate in advance whether the house dwellers will meet good or bad luck and, if well cared for, sometimes leave gifts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c12",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "The friendly, childishly gay, playful little dwarves love to help humans unseen, be it that they tend to fields and cattle or keep watch on children whose parents are absent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kurcaci kecil yang ramah itu suka membantu manusia tanpa terlihat, mengurus ladang dan ternak atau menjaga anak yang ditinggal orang tuanya.",
        "en": "The friendly little dwarves like to help humans unseen, tending fields and cattle or watching children whose parents are away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c13",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s1",
      "quote": "One day, though, a serious man came from afar who told the people that Perchta isn't to be trusted as the Heimchen are the souls of unbaptized children. When the people then avoided the Heimchen, Perchta decided that it was time to leave, and she and the Heimchen crossed the stream never to return again.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seorang lelaki dari jauh berkata Perchta tak dapat dipercaya karena Heimchen arwah anak belum dibaptis; ketika warga menjauhi Heimchen, Perchta dan Heimchen menyeberangi sungai dan tak kembali.",
        "en": "A man from afar said Perchta was not to be trusted since the Heimchen were souls of unbaptized children; when people shunned the Heimchen, Perchta and the Heimchen crossed the stream and never returned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c14",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s2",
      "quote": "Im Volksglauben galt die Grille im Haus als Glücksbringer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat (Volksglauben), jangkrik (Grille) di dalam rumah dianggap pembawa keberuntungan (Glücksbringer).",
        "en": "In folk belief (Volksglauben) the cricket (Grille) in the house was held to be a bringer of luck (Glücksbringer)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heimchen-c15",
      "source_id": "heimchen-s2",
      "quote": "Das Heimchen (Acheta domesticus), auch Hausgrille, ist eine Langfühlerschrecke aus der Familie der Echten Grillen (Gryllidae).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heimchen (Acheta domesticus), juga disebut Hausgrille, adalah serangga dari famili jangkrik sejati (Gryllidae).",
        "en": "The Heimchen (Acheta domesticus), also called Hausgrille, is an insect of the true cricket family (Gryllidae)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Hanya artikel en yang membahas Heimchen sebagai makhluk cerita rakyat; artikel de membahas serangganya dan hanya menyebut kepercayaan rakyat singkat. Karya Bechstein yang dirujuk tidak tersedia sebagai teks yang dapat diunduh."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Heintjevaar adalah roh air (watergeest) dalam cerita rakyat Texel yang dipercaya menyeret anak-anak ke dalam air.",
    "en": "Heintjevaar is a water spirit of Texel folklore said to drag children into the water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "heintjevaar-c01",
      "heintjevaar-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Heintjevaar adalah roh air (watergeest) yang menurut cerita rakyat Texel (Texelse) berdiam di perairan di sekitar dan di pulau Texel. Ia secara khusus merupakan sosok penakut anak: konon ia mencengkeram pergelangan kaki anak yang berjalan terlalu dekat dengan tepi air, lalu menyeret mereka ke kedalaman hingga tenggelam. Menurut mitos ini ia dapat ditemukan di laut, tetapi juga di parit, sumur, dan kolam dalam (kolken) yang banyak terdapat di padang rumput Texel.",
      "en": "Heintjevaar is a water spirit that, in the folk tales of Texel, dwells in the waters around and on the island. It is specifically a bogey for children: it is said to seize children by the ankles when they walk too close to the water's edge and drag them into the depths to drown. According to the myth it can be found in the sea, but also in ditches, wells and the deep pools (kolken) common in Texel's meadows.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heintjevaar-c01",
        "heintjevaar-c02",
        "heintjevaar-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam surat pembaca di Texelse Courant pada tahun 1937, Kees Hana menulis bahwa pada 1890 seorang petani menggali sumur di Eierland, dekat hoeve Vianen di Zwinweg. Tak lama kemudian beredar cerita hantu tentang sumur itu, yang konon menjadi asal mitos Heintjevaar, dan sumur itu dijuluki Heintjevaarsput. Sosok ini juga muncul dalam buku anak Annetje Lie in het holst van de nacht (1987) karya Imme Dros dan film adaptasinya tahun 2004.",
      "en": "In a letter to the Texelse Courant in 1937, Kees Hana wrote that in 1890 a farmer dug a well in Eierland near the Vianen farm on the Zwinweg. Ghost stories soon arose about the well, from which the Heintjevaar myth supposedly came, and the well was nicknamed Heintjevaarsput. The figure also appears in Imme Dros's children's book Annetje Lie in het holst van de nacht (1987) and its 2004 film.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "heintjevaar-c04",
        "heintjevaar-c05",
        "heintjevaar-c06",
        "heintjevaar-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c01",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "Heintjevaar is een watergeest die zich volgens Texelse volksverhalen zou ophouden in de wateren rond en op Texel.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heintjevaar adalah roh air (watergeest) yang menurut cerita rakyat Texel (Texelse volksverhalen) berdiam di perairan sekitar dan di Texel.",
        "en": "Heintjevaar is a water spirit (watergeest) that in Texel folk tales (Texelse volksverhalen) dwells in the waters around and on Texel."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c02",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "Heintjevaar is een watergeest en specifiek een kinderschrik. Hij zou kinderen bij hun enkels grijpen als ze te dicht bij de waterrand liepen, om ze mee in het diepe te sleuren en te laten verdrinken.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heintjevaar adalah sosok penakut anak (kinderschrik) yang mencengkeram pergelangan kaki (enkels) anak di tepi air (waterrand) dan menyeret mereka hingga tenggelam.",
        "en": "Heintjevaar is a child bogey (kinderschrik) who grabs children by the ankles (enkels) at the water's edge (waterrand) and drags them down to drown."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c03",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "Heintjevaar is volgens de mythe te vinden in de zee, maar ook in sloten, putten en kolken. Met name kolken komen op Texel veel in de weilanden voor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitos (mythe), Heintjevaar ada di laut (zee), parit (sloten), sumur (putten), dan kolken, yang banyak terdapat di padang rumput Texel.",
        "en": "According to the myth (mythe), Heintjevaar is found in the sea (zee), ditches (sloten), wells (putten) and kolken, common in Texel meadows."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c04",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "In 1890 zou er in Eierland, bij de hoeve Vianen aan de Zwinweg, door de boer van het land een put zijn gegraven (mogelijk het gebied Hogezandskil).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1890 seorang petani (boer) menggali sumur (put) di Eierland dekat hoeve Vianen di Zwinweg, mungkin di daerah Hogezandskil.",
        "en": "In 1890 a farmer (boer) dug a well (put) in Eierland by the Vianen farm (hoeve) on the Zwinweg, possibly the Hogezandskil area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c05",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "Al snel na de aanleg van deze put werden er spookverhalen over de put verteld, waar de mythe van Heintjevaar uit zou zijn ontstaan. De put zou volgens Hana de bijnaam \"Heintjevaarsput\" hebben gekregen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak lama kemudian muncul cerita hantu (spookverhalen) tentang sumur itu, asal mitos Heintjevaar; menurut Hana sumur itu dijuluki Heintjevaarsput.",
        "en": "Ghost stories (spookverhalen) soon arose about the well, the supposed origin of the Heintjevaar myth; according to Hana it was nicknamed Heintjevaarsput."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "heintjevaar-c06",
      "source_id": "heintjevaar-s1",
      "quote": "Het figuur Heintjevaar heeft ook een rol in het kinderboek Annetje Lie in het holst van de nacht (1987) van Imme Dros, die het boek deels op haar eigen jeugdherinneringen heeft gebaseerd. De figuur is ook te zien in de verfilming van het boek uit 2004.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Heintjevaar berperan dalam buku anak (kinderboek) Annetje Lie in het holst van de nacht (1987) karya Imme Dros dan dalam film adaptasinya (verfilming) tahun 2004.",
        "en": "Heintjevaar has a role in Imme Dros's children's book (kinderboek) Annetje Lie in het holst van de nacht (1987) and its 2004 film adaptation (verfilming)."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hemann adalah roh dalam cerita rakyat Jerman (German folklore) yang menakut-nakuti orang dengan teriakan, biasanya tanpa terlihat dan pada malam hari.",
    "en": "The Hemann is a spirit of German folklore that frightens people by yelling, usually unseen and at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hemann-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hemann, juga disebut Homann, Hoymann, Hoimann, atau Jochhoimann (Mann berarti lelaki), adalah roh dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang menakut-nakuti orang dengan teriakan, biasanya tanpa terlihat dan pada malam hari. Bagian pertama namanya biasanya meniru bunyi teriakannya. Ia dikenal di bekas wilayah berbahasa Jerman di Bohemia, tempat orang Ceko (Czechs) menyebutnya Hejkadlo, serta di Silesia, Westphalia, Vogtland, Upper Palatinate, Fichtel Mountains, dan dekat Crailsheim (Baden-Württemberg).",
      "en": "The Hemann, also Homann, Hoymann, Hoimann or Jochhoimann (Mann meaning man), is a spirit of German folklore that frightens people by yelling, usually unseen and at night. The first part of the name usually imitates its cry. It is known in formerly German-speaking Bohemia, where the Czechs call it Hejkadlo, as well as in Silesia, Westphalia, the Vogtland, the Upper Palatinate, the Fichtel Mountains and near Crailsheim (Baden-Württemberg).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hemann-c01",
        "hemann-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hemann berteriak \"he he!\" atau \"he helfts!\" (hai, tolong!) dan mengejar orang dengan teriakannya tanpa pernah benar-benar menyusul mereka. Namun siapa pun yang berani meniru teriakannya akan ditunggangi: roh itu melompat ke punggungnya dan memaksanya menggendong. Ia dianggap hantu, terutama Grenzfrevler, orang yang semasa hidup memindahkan batu batas dengan niat jahat, atau roh ladang yang diberi berkas panen terakhir. Asal-usulnya kemungkinan suara burung hantu, burung hantu besar, burung hupo, dan cabak.",
      "en": "The Hemann cries \"he he!\" or \"he helfts!\" (hey, help!) and chases people with its yelling without ever quite reaching them. But whoever dares to imitate its cry is leapt upon: the spirit jumps onto their back and forces them to carry it. It is held to be a ghost, especially a Grenzfrevler, someone who in life moved a boundary stone with criminal intent, or a field sprite offered the last sheaf of the harvest. It probably arose from the calls of the owl, eagle owl, hoopoe and nightjar.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hemann-c03",
        "hemann-c04",
        "hemann-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Upper Palatinate, Hoymann atau Hoimann menghantui hutan pegunungan; suaranya, kasar seperti lelaki atau seperti burung hantu, terdengar sampai seperempat jam jauhnya. Ia tampak sebagai lelaki jangkung berpakaian pemburu dengan topi lebar, atau sebagai raksasa yang menjulang ke awan, dan dianggap arwah malang atau Iblis sendiri. Ia menghukum pencurian kayu dan peniruan teriakannya, dan juga ikut dalam Wild Hunt.",
      "en": "In the Upper Palatinate the Hoymann or Hoimann haunts the mountain forests; its shout, coarse like a man's voice or like an owl, carries a quarter hour away. It appears as a tall man in hunter's dress with a broad-brimmed hat, or as a giant reaching the clouds, and is held to be a poor soul or the devil himself. It punishes wood theft and the imitation of its cry, and also rides with the Wild Hunt.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hemann-c05",
        "hemann-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ada banyak bentuk setempat. Di Lower Lusatia, Homann berteriak setiap tengah hari pukul 12 sehingga dipakai sebagai penanda waktu. Di Northern Bohemia, Hehmann tinggal di ladang gandum dan menunggangi leher orang yang membalas teriakannya. Di Westphalia ia disebut Ropenkerl, dan teriakan \"hoho!\" miliknya menjadi pertanda cuaca buruk. Di Lechrain, Hojemännlein adalah Kobold kecil berwarna hijau yang berteriak \"hoje!\". Dalam cerita rakyat Ceko, makhluk serupa disebut hejkal atau hejkadlo, iblis hutan yang juga melompat ke punggung orang yang membalas panggilannya.",
      "en": "There are many local forms. In Lower Lusatia the Homann yells every noon at 12 and so serves as a time signal. In Northern Bohemia the Hehmann lives in the cornfield and leaps on the neck of anyone who answers its shout. In Westphalia it is called Ropenkerl, and its cry \"hoho!\" foretells bad weather. In the Lechrain the Hojemännlein are small green Kobolds who yell \"hoje!\". In Czech folklore a similar being is called hejkal or hejkadlo, a forest demon that likewise jumps onto the back of anyone who answers its call.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hemann-c01",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "The Hemann ( German: [ˈheːˌman] ), also Homann, Hoymann, Hoimann, or Jochhoimann, (Mann = man), is a spirit from German folklore known to scare people through yelling, usually invisibly and at night.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hemann, juga Homann, Hoymann, Hoimann, atau Jochhoimann, adalah roh cerita rakyat Jerman yang menakut-nakuti orang dengan teriakan, biasanya tak terlihat dan pada malam hari.",
        "en": "The Hemann, also Homann, Hoymann, Hoimann or Jochhoimann, is a German folklore spirit that scares people by yelling, usually invisibly and at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c02",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "It can be found in former German-speaking Bohemia (where the Czechs also know it as Hejkadlo), former German-speaking Silesia, Upper Palatinate, the Fichtel Mountains, the Vogtland, Westphalia, and around Crailsheim in Baden-Württemberg.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dikenal di Bohemia (orang Czechs menyebutnya Hejkadlo), Silesia, Upper Palatinate, Fichtel Mountains, Vogtland, Westphalia, dan sekitar Crailsheim di Baden-Württemberg.",
        "en": "It is found in Bohemia (where the Czechs call it Hejkadlo), Silesia, the Upper Palatinate, the Fichtel Mountains, the Vogtland, Westphalia and around Crailsheim in Baden-Württemberg."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c03",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "The Hemann yells \"he he!\" or \"he helfts!\" (\"Hey help!\") and chases the people with its yelling without ever reaching them completely. Only, it will \"aufhocken\" (leap upon a person's back and force them to carry the aufhocking spirit) if anybody dares to imitate its yells.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hemann berteriak \"he he!\" atau \"he helfts!\" dan mengejar orang tanpa pernah menyusul; ia melakukan aufhocken, melompat ke punggung, bila ada yang meniru teriakannya.",
        "en": "The Hemann yells \"he he!\" or \"he helfts!\" and chases people without reaching them; it will aufhocken, leap on a person's back, if anyone imitates its yells."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c04",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "It is either a ghost, especially a Grenzfrevler (boundary sinner, i.e. someone who has moved a boundary stone or similar landmark with criminal intent, thus damning the soul to haunt the very boundary it unlawfully changed in life), or a field sprite who is offered the last sheaf of harvest.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia adalah hantu, terutama Grenzfrevler yang memindahkan batu batas dengan niat jahat, atau roh ladang yang diberi berkas panen terakhir.",
        "en": "It is either a ghost, especially a Grenzfrevler who moved a boundary stone with criminal intent, or a field sprite offered the last sheaf of harvest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c05",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "In Upper Palatinate, there is the Hoymann or Hoimann haunting the mountain forests besides the Hemann proper. Its shout, sounding like a coarse male voice or like an owl, can still be heard a quarter hour away.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Upper Palatinate ada Hoymann atau Hoimann yang menghantui hutan pegunungan; teriakannya terdengar sampai seperempat jam jauhnya.",
        "en": "In the Upper Palatinate the Hoymann or Hoimann haunts the mountain forests; its shout can be heard a quarter hour away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c06",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "Its identity is either an irredeemable poor soul of a deceased (German arme Seele) or the devil (German Teufel) himself. The Hoymann punishes the theft of wood from the forest and the imitation of its shout, the latter with \"aufhocken\", leading astray, or a slap in the face. The Hoimann also accompanies the Wild Hunt",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hoymann adalah arwah malang (arme Seele) atau Iblis (Teufel); ia menghukum pencurian kayu dan peniruan teriakannya, dan Hoimann ikut dalam Wild Hunt.",
        "en": "The Hoymann is a poor soul (arme Seele) or the devil (Teufel); it punishes wood theft and imitation of its shout, and the Hoimann accompanies the Wild Hunt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c07",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "In Lower Lusatia, the Homann yells every noon at stroke 12 p.m. and is thus used as a time indicator by the local people.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Lower Lusatia, Homann berteriak setiap tengah hari pukul 12 dan dipakai warga sebagai penanda waktu.",
        "en": "In Lower Lusatia the Homann yells every noon at 12 and serves locals as a time indicator."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c08",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "In Northern Bohemia, the Hehmann dwells in the cornfield and shrilly shouts \"heh\". Those who dare to answer with the same shout will experience \"aufhocken\". The Hehmann leaps on their neck.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Northern Bohemia, Hehmann tinggal di ladang gandum dan menunggangi leher orang yang membalas teriakannya.",
        "en": "In Northern Bohemia the Hehmann dwells in the cornfield and leaps on the neck of those who answer its shout."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c09",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "In Westphalia, where the Hemann is known as Ropenkerl (shouting guy), his shout \"hoho!\" is an omen of bad weather.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Westphalia Hemann disebut Ropenkerl, dan teriakan \"hoho!\" miliknya pertanda cuaca buruk.",
        "en": "In Westphalia the Hemann is called Ropenkerl, and its shout \"hoho!\" is an omen of bad weather."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c10",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "In the Lechrain, the Hojemännlein, Hojemännl, Hojemännel, or Hojemannl (sg., pl.; Männlein, Männl or Mannl = manikin) are little Kobolds, tiny and green of appearance",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Lechrain, Hojemännlein adalah Kobold kecil berwarna hijau.",
        "en": "In the Lechrain the Hojemännlein are little green Kobolds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c11",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "There further is the Heitmännchen (Männchen = manikin) shouting \"heit heit!\" or \"ju ju!\". It has the appearance of a little blue flame, will \"aufhocken\" if imitated but also helps with the harvest when offered a sandwich.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ada pula Heitmännchen yang berupa nyala api biru kecil, menunggangi peniru, tetapi membantu panen bila diberi roti.",
        "en": "There is also the Heitmännchen, a little blue flame that leaps on imitators but helps with the harvest when given a sandwich."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c12",
      "source_id": "hemann-s1",
      "quote": "The Hemann likely has its origin in the very real calls of the owl and eagle owl, the hoopoe, and the nightjar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hemann kemungkinan berasal dari suara burung hantu, burung hantu besar, burung hupo, dan cabak.",
        "en": "The Hemann likely originates in the calls of the owl, eagle owl, hoopoe and nightjar."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c13",
      "source_id": "hemann-s2",
      "quote": "Hejkal je v českém a slovanském bájesloví lesní démon, nazývaný také hýkal či hejkadlo, nebo divý muž.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Ceko (českém), hejkal adalah iblis hutan (lesní démon), juga disebut hýkal atau hejkadlo.",
        "en": "In Czech (českém) mythology the hejkal is a forest demon (lesní démon), also called hýkal or hejkadlo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hemann-c14",
      "source_id": "hemann-s2",
      "quote": "Podle pověstí je zvláště nebezpečné hejkalovo volání opětovat, ten poté skočí člověku na záda a prožene ho po lese či jej roztrhá.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda (pověstí), membalas panggilan hejkal (hejkalovo volání) berbahaya: ia lalu melompat ke punggung (záda) orang itu.",
        "en": "According to legend (pověstí), answering the hejkal's call (hejkalovo volání) is dangerous: it then jumps on the person's back (záda)."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rusa Hercynia (Hercynisch hert) adalah salah satu hewan Hutan Hercynia (Hercynische Woud) yang dideskripsikan Julius Caesar: lembu berbentuk rusa dengan satu tanduk di dahi.",
    "en": "The Hercynian stag (Hercynisch hert) is one of the animals of the Hercynian Forest described by Julius Caesar: an ox shaped like a stag with a single horn on its forehead.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hercynisch-hert-c01",
      "hercynisch-hert-c03",
      "hercynisch-hert-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Rusa Hercynia (Hercynisch hert) adalah salah satu hewan dari Hutan Hercynia (Hercynische Woud) yang dideskripsikan Julius Caesar. Dalam De Bello Gallico, Caesar menceritakan seekor rusa raksasa, sebesar lembu, di wilayah yang kini menjadi Jerman (Duitsland); orang Romawi (Romeinen) memang mudah menyebut hewan besar berbentuk lembu. Caesar menulis bahwa hewan itu adalah lembu berbentuk rusa, dengan satu tanduk yang tumbuh dari tengah dahi di antara kedua telinganya, lebih tinggi dan lebih lurus daripada tanduk yang dikenal, dan dari ujungnya bercabang-cabang seperti daun palem. Bentuk jantan dan betinanya sama.",
      "en": "The Hercynian stag (Hercynisch hert) is one of the animals of the Hercynian Forest (Hercynische Woud) described by Julius Caesar. In De Bello Gallico, Caesar told of a gigantic stag, as large as an ox, in the region that is now Germany (Duitsland); the Romans (Romeinen) readily called large animals ox-shaped. Caesar wrote that it was an ox shaped like a stag, with a single horn rising from the middle of the forehead between the ears, taller and straighter than the horns known to him, branching at the top like palms. Male and female have the same shape.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hercynisch-hert-c01",
        "hercynisch-hert-c02",
        "hercynisch-hert-c03",
        "hercynisch-hert-c04",
        "hercynisch-hert-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para sejarawan menyimpulkan bahwa hewan ini pastilah elk (eland), yang dulu hidup lebih jauh ke selatan daripada sekarang. Rusa Hercynia kemudian masuk ke dalam tradisi cerita besar tentang unicorn (eenhoorns) di Jerman, yang asal-usulnya berakar pada kebudayaan Yunani (Griekse).",
      "en": "Historians have concluded that this must have been an elk (eland), which once lived farther south than today. The Hercynian stag later entered a large narrative tradition in Germany about unicorns (eenhoorns) whose origins lay in Greek (Griekse) culture.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hercynisch-hert-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
